<p><b>Luxembourg</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ʌ/: &#39;u&#39; in &#39;cut&#39;">ʌ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/əm/: &#39;m&#39; in &#39;rhythm&#39;">əm</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ɜːr/: &#39;ur&#39; in &#39;fur&#39;">ɜːr</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Luxembourg.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/7a/En-us-Luxembourg.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Luxembourg.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">LUK</span>-səm-burg</i></a>;<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourgish language">Luxembourgish</a>: <i lang="lb">Lëtzebuerg</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="lb-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Luxembourgish" title="Help:IPA/Luxembourgish">[ˈlətsəbuəɕ]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Lb-L%C3%ABtzebuerg.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/42/Lb-L%C3%ABtzebuerg.ogg" class="internal" title="Lb-Lëtzebuerg.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>; French: <i lang="fr">Luxembourg</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[lyksɑ̃buʁ]</a></span>; German: <i lang="de">Luxemburg</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="de-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Standard_German" title="Help:IPA/Standard German">[ˈlʊksm̩bʊʁk]</a></span>), officially the <b>Grand Duchy of Luxembourg</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> is a small <a href="/wiki/Landlocked" class="mw-redirect" title="Landlocked">landlocked</a> country in <a href="/wiki/Western_Europe" title="Western Europe">Western Europe</a>. It borders <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a> to the west and north, <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a> to the east, and <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> to the south. Its capital and most populous city, <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> is one of <a href="/wiki/Institutional_seats_of_the_European_Union" title="Institutional seats of the European Union">the four institutional seats of the European Union</a> (together with <a href="/wiki/City_of_Brussels" title="City of Brussels">Brussels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Frankfurt" title="Frankfurt">Frankfurt</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Strasbourg" title="Strasbourg">Strasbourg</a>) and the seat of several EU institutions, notably the <a href="/wiki/Court_of_Justice_of_the_European_Union" title="Court of Justice of the European Union">Court of Justice of the European Union</a>, the highest judicial authority.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg's culture, people, and languages are highly intertwined with its <a href="/wiki/French_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="French culture">French</a> and <a href="/wiki/German_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="German culture">German</a> neighbors; while <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish" title="Luxembourgish">Luxembourgish</a> is the only national language of the <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgers" title="Luxembourgers">Luxembourgish people</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-auto_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> is the only legal language, and all three — <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish" title="Luxembourgish">Luxembourgish</a>, <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> and <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a> — are considered official languages and are used for administrative matters in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With an area of 2,586 square kilometers (998&#160;sq&#160;mi), Luxembourg is one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_area" title="List of European countries by area">smallest countries in Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-europa1_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-europa1-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> In 2022, it had a population of 645,397, which makes it one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_population" title="List of European countries by population">least-populated countries in Europe</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> albeit with the <a href="/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_population_growth_rate" title="List of European countries by population growth rate">highest population growth rate</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> foreigners account for nearly half the population.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg is a <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democracy</a> headed by a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarch" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitutional monarch">constitutional monarch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Henri" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duke Henri">Grand Duke Henri</a>, making it the world's only remaining sovereign <a href="/wiki/Grand_duchy" title="Grand duchy">grand duchy</a>.
</p><p>Luxembourg is a <a href="/wiki/Developed_country" title="Developed country">developed country</a> with an advanced economy and one of the world's highest <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">GDP (PPP) per capita</a>. The city of Luxembourg was declared a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a> in 1994 due to the exceptional preservation of its vast fortifications and historic quarters.<sup id="cite_ref-UNESCO,_World_Heritage_Convention_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNESCO,_World_Heritage_Convention-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg is a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development" class="mw-redirect" title="Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development">OECD</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Benelux" title="Benelux">Benelux</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:1_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> It served on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council" title="United Nations Security Council">United Nations Security Council</a> for the first time in 2013 and 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Luxembourg" title="History of Luxembourg">History of Luxembourg</a></div><p>The history of Luxembourg is considered to begin in the year <a href="/wiki/963" title="963">963</a>, when <a href="/wiki/Count" title="Count">Count</a> <a href="/wiki/Sigfried,_Count_of_the_Ardennes" title="Sigfried, Count of the Ardennes">Siegfried</a> acquired a rocky promontory and its Roman-era fortifications, known as <i>Lucilinburhuc</i>, "little castle", and the surrounding area from the <a href="/wiki/St._Maximin%27s_Abbey,_Trier" title="St. Maximin&#39;s Abbey, Trier">Imperial Abbey of St. Maximin</a> in nearby <a href="/wiki/Trier" title="Trier">Trier</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Siegfried's descendants increased their territory through marriage, conquest, and <a href="/wiki/Vassal" title="Vassal">vassalage</a>. By the end of the 13th century, the <a href="/wiki/Count_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Count of Luxembourg">counts of Luxembourg</a> reigned over a considerable territory.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In 1308, <a href="/wiki/Henry_VII,_Count_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry VII, Count of Luxembourg">Count of Luxembourg Henry VII</a> became <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_Romans" title="King of the Romans">King of the Romans</a> and later <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Luxembourg" title="House of Luxembourg">House of Luxembourg</a> would produce four Holy Roman Emperors during the High Middle Ages. In 1354, <a href="/wiki/Charles_IV,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles IV, Holy Roman Emperor">Charles IV</a> elevated the county to the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Duchy of Luxembourg">Duchy of Luxembourg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Luxembourg_-_History_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luxembourg_-_History-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The duchy eventually became part of the <a href="/wiki/Burgundian_Circle" title="Burgundian Circle">Burgundian Circle</a> and then one of the <a href="/wiki/Seventeen_Provinces" title="Seventeen Provinces">Seventeen Provinces</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_Netherlands" title="Habsburg Netherlands">Habsburg Netherlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Over the centuries, the City and <a href="/wiki/Fortress_of_Luxembourg" title="Fortress of Luxembourg">Fortress of Luxembourg</a>—of great strategic importance due to its location between the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">Kingdom of France</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Habsburg">Habsburg</a> territories—was gradually built up to be one of the most reputed <a href="/wiki/Fortifications" class="mw-redirect" title="Fortifications">fortifications</a> in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> After belonging to both the France of <a href="/wiki/Louis_XIV" title="Louis XIV">Louis XIV</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a> of <a href="/wiki/Maria_Theresa" title="Maria Theresa">Maria Theresa</a>, Luxembourg became part of the <a href="/wiki/First_French_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="First French Republic">First French Republic</a> and <a href="/wiki/First_French_Empire" title="First French Empire">Empire</a> under <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The present-day state of Luxembourg first emerged at the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna" title="Congress of Vienna">Congress of Vienna</a> in 1815. The Grand Duchy, with its powerful fortress, became an independent state under the personal possession of <a href="/wiki/William_I_of_the_Netherlands" title="William I of the Netherlands">William I of the Netherlands</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Prussia" title="Kingdom of Prussia">Prussian</a> garrison to guard the city against another invasion from France.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Luxembourg_-_History_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luxembourg_-_History-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> In 1839, following the turmoil of the <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Revolution" title="Belgian Revolution">Belgian Revolution</a>, the purely French-speaking part of Luxembourg was ceded to <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a> and the Luxembourgish-speaking part (except the <a href="/wiki/Arelerland" title="Arelerland">Arelerland</a>, the area around <a href="/wiki/Arlon" title="Arlon">Arlon</a>) became what is the present state of Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Before_AD_963">Before AD 963</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Before AD 963">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Celtic_Luxembourg" title="Celtic Luxembourg">Celtic Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg/170px-Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="266" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg/255px-Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg/340px-Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1728" data-file-height="2703" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Codex_aureus_Epternacensis_folio_24_2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Text page from the <a href="/wiki/Codex_Aureus_of_Echternach" title="Codex Aureus of Echternach">Codex Aureus of Echternach</a>, an important surviving codex, was produced in the <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Echternach" title="Abbey of Echternach">Abbey of Echternach</a> in the 11th century.<sup id="cite_ref-Beckwith,_John_1979_pp._122_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beckwith,_John_1979_pp._122-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The first traces of settlement in what is now Luxembourg are dated back to the <a href="/wiki/Paleolithic_Age" class="mw-redirect" title="Paleolithic Age">Paleolithic Age</a>, about 35,000 years ago. From the 2nd century BC, <a href="/wiki/Celtic_tribes" class="mw-redirect" title="Celtic tribes">Celtic tribes</a> settled in the region between the rivers <a href="/wiki/Rhine" title="Rhine">Rhine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Meuse" title="Meuse">Meuse</a>, thus settling in the region which constitutes today's Grand-Duchy.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Six centuries later, the <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Rome" title="Ancient Rome">Romans</a> would name the Celtic tribes inhabiting these exact regions collectively as the <i><a href="/wiki/Treveri" title="Treveri">Treveri</a></i>. Multiple examples of archeological evidence proving their existence in Luxembourg have been discovered, the most famous being the "<a href="/wiki/Titelberg" title="Titelberg">Oppidum of the Titelberg</a>".
</p><p>In around 58 to 51 BC, the Romans invaded the country when <a href="/wiki/Julius_Caesar" title="Julius Caesar">Julius Caesar</a> <a href="/wiki/Gallic_Wars" title="Gallic Wars">conquered Gaul</a> and part of Germania up to the Rhine border, thus the area of what is now Luxembourg became part of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman Empire</a> for the next 450 years, living in relative peace under the <a href="/wiki/Pax_Romana" title="Pax Romana">Pax Romana</a>.
</p><p>Similar to what happened in Gaul, the Celts of Luxembourg adopted Roman culture, language, morals and a way of life, effectively becoming what historians later described as <a href="/wiki/Gallo-Roman_culture" title="Gallo-Roman culture">Gallo-Roman civilization</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Evidence from that period of time includes the <a href="/wiki/Dalheim_Ricciacum" title="Dalheim Ricciacum">Dalheim Ricciacum</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Vichten" title="Vichten">Vichten mosaic</a> which is on display at the <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_History_and_Art" title="National Museum of History and Art">National Museum of History and Art</a> in Luxembourg City.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The territory was infiltrated by the <a href="/wiki/Germanic_peoples" title="Germanic peoples">Germanic</a> <a href="/wiki/Franks" title="Franks">Franks</a> starting from the 4th century, and was abandoned by Rome in AD 406.<sup id="cite_ref-Trausch2003_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Trausch2003-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 65">&#58;&#8202;65&#8202;</span></sup> The territory of what would become Luxembourg now became part of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Franks" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of the Franks">Kingdom of the Franks</a>. The Salian Franks who settled in the area are often described as the ones having brought the Germanic language to present-day Luxembourg, since the <a href="/wiki/Frankish_language" title="Frankish language">old Frankish</a> language spoken by them is considered by linguists to be a direct forerunner of the <a href="/wiki/Moselle_Franconian_language" title="Moselle Franconian language">Moselle Franconian dialect</a>, which later evolved, among others, into the modern-day <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish" title="Luxembourgish">Luxembourgish language</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Trausch2003_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Trausch2003-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 70">&#58;&#8202;70&#8202;</span></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Christianization" title="Christianization">Christianization</a> of Luxembourg also falls into this epoch and is usually dated back to the end of the 7th century. The most famous figure in this context is <a href="/wiki/Willibrord" title="Willibrord">Willibrord</a>, a Northumbrian missionary saint, who together with other monks established the <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Echternach" title="Abbey of Echternach">Abbey of Echternach</a> in AD 698.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
It is in his honor that the notable <a href="/wiki/Dancing_procession_of_Echternach" title="Dancing procession of Echternach">Dancing procession of Echternach</a> takes place annually on Whit Tuesday. For a few centuries, the abbey would become one of northern Europe's most influential abbeys. The <a href="/wiki/Codex_Aureus_of_Echternach" title="Codex Aureus of Echternach">Codex Aureus of Echternach</a>, an important surviving codex written entirely in gold ink, was produced here in the 11th century.<sup id="cite_ref-Beckwith,_John_1979_pp._122_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Beckwith,_John_1979_pp._122-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The so-called <a href="/wiki/Emperor%27s_Bible" title="Emperor&#39;s Bible">Emperor's Bible</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Gospels_of_Henry_III" title="Golden Gospels of Henry III">Golden Gospels of Henry III</a> were also produced in Echternach at this time, when production of books at the <a href="/wiki/Scriptorium" title="Scriptorium">scriptorium</a> peaked during the middle-age.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 9–25">&#58;&#8202;9–25&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Emergence_and_expansion_of_the_County_of_Luxemburg_.28963.E2.80.931312.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Emergence_and_expansion_of_the_County_of_Luxemburg_(963–1312)">Emergence and expansion of the County of Luxemburg (963–1312)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Emergence and expansion of the County of Luxemburg (963–1312)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/County_of_Luxemburg" title="County of Luxemburg">County of Luxemburg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg/170px-Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="253" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg/255px-Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg/340px-Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2045" data-file-height="3043" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_IV-John_Ocko_votive_picture-fragment.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Charles_IV,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles IV, Holy Roman Emperor">Charles IV</a>, the 14th-century Holy Roman Emperor and King of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Bohemia" title="Kingdom of Bohemia">Bohemia</a> from the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Luxembourg" title="House of Luxembourg">House of Luxembourg</a><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>When the <a href="/wiki/Carolingian_Empire" title="Carolingian Empire">Carolingian Empire</a> was divided multiple times starting with the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Verdun" title="Treaty of Verdun">Treaty of Verdun</a> in 843, today's Luxembourgish territory became successively part of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Middle_Francia" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Middle Francia">Kingdom of Middle Francia</a> (843–855), the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lotharingia" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Lotharingia">Kingdom of Lotharingia</a> (855–959) and finally of the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Lorraine" title="Duchy of Lorraine">Duchy of Lorraine</a> (959–1059), which itself had become a state of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The recorded history of Luxembourg begins with the acquisition of <i>Lucilinburhuc</i><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> (today <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Castle" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg Castle">Luxembourg Castle</a>) situated on the <a href="/wiki/Bock_(Luxembourg)" title="Bock (Luxembourg)">Bock</a> rock by <a href="/wiki/Siegfried_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Siegfried of Luxembourg">Siegfried, Count of the Ardennes</a>, in 963 through an exchange act with <a href="/wiki/St._Maximin%27s_Abbey,_Trier" title="St. Maximin&#39;s Abbey, Trier">St. Maximin's Abbey, Trier</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Around this <a href="/wiki/Fortifications" class="mw-redirect" title="Fortifications">fort</a>, a town gradually developed, which became the center of a state of great strategic value within the Duchy of Lorraine.<sup id="cite_ref-UNESCO,_World_Heritage_Convention_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNESCO,_World_Heritage_Convention-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Over the years, the fortress was extended by Siegfried's descendants and by 1083, one of them, <a href="/wiki/Conrad_I,_Count_of_Luxembourg" title="Conrad I, Count of Luxembourg">Conrad I</a>, was the first to call himself a "<a href="/wiki/Count_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Count of Luxembourg">Count of Luxembourg</a>", and with it effectively creating the independent <a href="/wiki/County_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="County of Luxembourg">County of Luxembourg</a> (which was still a state within the Holy Roman Empire).<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By the middle of the 13th century, the counts of Luxembourg had managed to considerably gain in wealth and power, and had expanded their territory from the river <a href="/wiki/Meuse" title="Meuse">Meuse</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Moselle" title="Moselle">Moselle</a>. By the time of the reign of <a href="/wiki/Henry_V,_Count_of_Luxembourg" title="Henry V, Count of Luxembourg">Henry V the Blonde</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bitburg" title="Bitburg">Bitburg</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Roche-en-Ardenne" title="La Roche-en-Ardenne">La Roche-en-Ardenne</a>, <a href="/wiki/Durbuy" title="Durbuy">Durbuy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arlon" title="Arlon">Arlon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Thionville" title="Thionville">Thionville</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marville,_Meuse" title="Marville, Meuse">Marville</a>, <a href="/wiki/Longwy" title="Longwy">Longwy</a>, and in 1264 the competing <a href="/wiki/County_of_Vianden" class="mw-redirect" title="County of Vianden">County of Vianden</a> (and with it <a href="/wiki/St._Vith" title="St. Vith">St. Vith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Schleiden" title="Schleiden">Schleiden</a>) had either been incorporated directly or become <a href="/wiki/Vassal_states" class="mw-redirect" title="Vassal states">vassal states</a> to the County of Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The only major setback during their rise in power came in 1288, when <a href="/wiki/Henry_VI,_Count_of_Luxembourg" title="Henry VI, Count of Luxembourg">Henry VI</a> and his three brothers died at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Worringen" title="Battle of Worringen">Battle of Worringen</a>, while trying unsuccessfully to also add the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Limburg" title="Duchy of Limburg">Duchy of Limburg</a> into their realm. But despite the defeat, the Battle of Worringen helped the Counts of Luxembourg to achieve military glory, which they had previously lacked, as they had mostly enlarged their territory by means of inheritances, marriages and fiefdoms.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The ascension of the Counts of Luxembourg culminated when <a href="/wiki/Henry_VII,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Henry VII, Holy Roman Emperor">Henry VII</a> became <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_Romans" title="King of the Romans">King of the Romans</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a> and finally, in 1312, <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Golden_Age:_The_House_of_Luxembourg_contending_for_supremacy_in_Central_Europe_.281312.E2.80.931443.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Golden_Age:_The_House_of_Luxembourg_contending_for_supremacy_in_Central_Europe_(1312–1443)">Golden Age: The House of Luxembourg contending for supremacy in Central Europe (1312–1443)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Golden Age: The House of Luxembourg contending for supremacy in Central Europe (1312–1443)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Luxemburg" title="Duchy of Luxemburg">Duchy of Luxemburg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg/220px-Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="185" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg/330px-Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg/440px-Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1830" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Festungsplan.von.Luxemburg.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historic map (undated) of Luxembourg City's fortifications</div></div></div>
<p>With the ascension of Henry VII as Emperor, the dynasty of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Luxembourg" title="House of Luxembourg">House of Luxembourg</a> not only began to rule the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>, but rapidly began to exercise growing influence over other parts of Central Europe as well.
</p><p>Henry's son, <a href="/wiki/John_the_Blind" class="mw-redirect" title="John the Blind">John the Blind</a>, in addition to being Count of Luxembourg, also became <a href="/wiki/King_of_Bohemia" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Bohemia">King of Bohemia</a>. He remains a major figure in Luxembourgish history and <a href="/wiki/Folklore" title="Folklore">folklore</a> and is considered by many historians the epitome of <a href="/wiki/Chivalry" title="Chivalry">chivalry</a> in medieval times. He is also known for having founded the <a href="/wiki/Schueberfouer" title="Schueberfouer">Schueberfouer</a> in 1340 and for his heroic death at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Cr%C3%A9cy" title="Battle of Crécy">Battle of Crécy</a> in 1346.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> John the Blind is considered a <a href="/wiki/Folk_hero" title="Folk hero">national hero</a> in Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 14th and early 15th centuries, three more members of the House of Luxembourg reigned as Holy Roman Emperors and Bohemian Kings: John's descendants <a href="/wiki/Charles_IV,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles IV, Holy Roman Emperor">Charles IV</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sigismund,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Sigismund, Holy Roman Emperor">Sigismund</a> (who also was <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary and Croatia</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Wenceslaus_IV_of_Bohemia" title="Wenceslaus IV of Bohemia">Wenceslaus IV</a>. Charles IV created the long-lasting <a href="/wiki/Golden_Bull_of_1356" title="Golden Bull of 1356">Golden Bull of 1356</a>, a decree which fixed important aspects of the constitutional structure of the Empire. Luxembourg remained an independent fief (county) of the Holy Roman Empire, and in 1354, Charles IV elevated it to the status of a <a href="/wiki/Duchy" title="Duchy">duchy</a> with his half-brother <a href="/wiki/Wenceslaus_I,_Duke_of_Luxembourg" title="Wenceslaus I, Duke of Luxembourg">Wenceslaus I</a> becoming the first <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Luxembourg">Duke of Luxembourg</a>. While his kin were occupied ruling and expanding their power within the Holy Roman Empire and elsewhere, Wenceslaus, annexed the <a href="/wiki/County_of_Chiny" class="mw-redirect" title="County of Chiny">County of Chiny</a> in 1364, and with it, the territories of the new <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Duchy of Luxembourg">Duchy of Luxembourg</a> reached its greatest extent.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During these 130 years, the House of Luxembourg was contending with the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg" title="House of Habsburg">House of Habsburg</a> for supremacy within the Holy Roman Empire and Central Europe. It all came to end in 1443, when the House of Luxembourg suffered a succession crisis, precipitated by the lack of a male heir to assume the throne. Since Sigismund and <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_of_G%C3%B6rlitz" title="Elizabeth of Görlitz">Elizabeth of Görlitz</a> were both heirless, all possessions of the Luxembourg Dynasty were redistributed among the European aristocracy.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The Duchy of Luxembourg become a possession of <a href="/wiki/Philip_the_Good" title="Philip the Good">Philip the Good</a>, <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Burgundy" title="Duke of Burgundy">Duke of Burgundy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the House of Luxembourg had become extinct and Luxembourg now became part of the <a href="/wiki/Burgundian_Netherlands" title="Burgundian Netherlands">Burgundian Netherlands</a>, this would mark the start of nearly 400 years of foreign rule over Luxembourg.
</p>
<h3><span id="Luxembourg_under_Habsburg_rule_and_repeated_French_invasions_.281444.E2.80.931794.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Luxembourg_under_Habsburg_rule_and_repeated_French_invasions_(1444–1794)">Luxembourg under Habsburg rule and repeated French invasions (1444–1794)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Luxembourg under Habsburg rule and repeated French invasions (1444–1794)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Fortress_of_Luxembourg" title="Fortress of Luxembourg">Fortress of Luxembourg</a></div>
<p>In 1482, <a href="/wiki/Philip_the_Handsome" class="mw-redirect" title="Philip the Handsome">Philip the Handsome</a> inherited all of what became then known as the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_Netherlands" title="Habsburg Netherlands">Habsburg Netherlands</a>, and with it the Duchy of Luxembourg. For nearly 320 years Luxembourg would remain a possession of the mighty House of Habsburg, at first under Austrian rule (1506–1556), then under <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Netherlands" title="Spanish Netherlands">Spanish rule (1556–1714)</a>, before going back again to <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Netherlands" title="Austrian Netherlands">Austrian rule (1714–1794)</a>.<br />
With having become a Habsburg possession, the Duchy of Luxembourg became, like many countries in Europe at the time, heavily involved into the many conflicts for dominance of Europe between the Habsburg-held countries and the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">Kingdom of France</a>.
</p><p>In 1542, the <a href="/wiki/King_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="King of France">King of France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Francis_I_of_France" title="Francis I of France">Francois I</a>, invaded Luxembourg twice, but the Habsburgs under <a href="/wiki/Charles_V,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles V, Holy Roman Emperor">Charles V</a> managed to reconquer the Duchy each time.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg became part of the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Netherlands" title="Spanish Netherlands">Spanish Netherlands</a> in 1556, and when France and Spain <a href="/wiki/Franco-Spanish_War_(1635%E2%80%931659)" title="Franco-Spanish War (1635–1659)">went to war in 1635</a> it resulted in the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_the_Pyrenees" title="Treaty of the Pyrenees">Treaty of the Pyrenees</a>, in which <a href="/wiki/Partitions_of_Luxembourg" title="Partitions of Luxembourg">the first partition of Luxembourg</a> was decided. Under the Treaty, Spain ceded the Luxembourgish fortresses of <a href="/wiki/Stenay" title="Stenay">Stenay</a>, Thionville, and <a href="/wiki/Montm%C3%A9dy" title="Montmédy">Montmédy</a>, and the surrounding territory to France, effectively reducing the size of Luxembourg for the first time in centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.57_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.57-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In context of the <a href="/wiki/Nine_Years%27_War" title="Nine Years&#39; War">Nine Years' War</a> in 1684, <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Luxembourg_(1684)" title="Siege of Luxembourg (1684)">France invaded Luxembourg again</a>, conquering and occupying the Duchy until 1697 when it was returned to the Spanish in order to garner support for the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Bourbon" title="House of Bourbon">Bourbon</a> cause during the prelude to the <a href="/wiki/War_of_the_Spanish_Succession" title="War of the Spanish Succession">War of the Spanish Succession</a>. When the war broke out in 1701 Luxembourg and the Spanish Netherlands were administered by the pro-French faction under the governor <a href="/wiki/Maximilian_II_Emanuel,_Elector_of_Bavaria" title="Maximilian II Emanuel, Elector of Bavaria">Maximilian II Emanuel, Elector of Bavaria</a> and sided with the Bourbons. The duchy was subsequently occupied by the pro-Austrian allied forces during the conflict and was awarded to Austria at its conclusion in 1714.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the Duchy of Luxembourg repeatedly passed back and forth from Spanish and Austrian to French rule, each of the conquering nations contributed to strengthening and expanding the <a href="/wiki/Fortress_of_Luxembourg" title="Fortress of Luxembourg">Fortress</a> that the Castle of Luxembourg had become over the years. One example of this includes French military engineer <a href="/wiki/Marquis_de_Vauban" class="mw-redirect" title="Marquis de Vauban">Marquis de Vauban</a> who advanced the fortifications around and on the heights of the city, fortification walls that are still visible today.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.57_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.57-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Luxembourg_under_French_rule_.281794.E2.80.931815.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Luxembourg_under_French_rule_(1794–1815)">Luxembourg under French rule (1794–1815)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Luxembourg under French rule (1794–1815)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/For%C3%AAts" title="Forêts">Forêts</a> and <a href="/wiki/Secularism_in_France" title="Secularism in France">Secularism in France</a></div>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/War_of_the_First_Coalition" title="War of the First Coalition">War of the First Coalition</a>, <a href="/wiki/First_French_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="First French Republic">Revolutionary France</a> invaded the Austrian Netherlands, and with it, Luxembourg, yet again. In the years 1793 and 1794 most of the Duchy was conquered relatively fast and the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolutionary_Army" title="French Revolutionary Army">French Revolutionary Army</a> committed many atrocities and pillages against the Luxembourgish civilian population and abbeys, the most infamous being the massacres of <a href="/wiki/Differdange" title="Differdange">Differdange</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dudelange" title="Dudelange">Dudelange</a>, as well as the destruction of the abbeys of <a href="/wiki/Clairefontaine_Abbey" title="Clairefontaine Abbey">Clairefontaine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Echternach" title="Abbey of Echternach">Echternach</a> and <a href="/wiki/Orval_Abbey" title="Orval Abbey">Orval</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> However the Fortress of Luxembourg <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Luxembourg_(1794%E2%80%9395)" class="mw-redirect" title="Siege of Luxembourg (1794–95)">resisted for nearly 7 months</a> before the Austrian forces holding it surrendered. Luxembourg's long defense led <a href="/wiki/Lazare_Carnot" title="Lazare Carnot">Lazare Carnot</a> to call Luxembourg "the best fortress in the world, except Gibraltar", giving rise to the city's nickname <i>the Gibraltar of the North</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Kreins_2003,_p.64_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kreins_2003,_p.64-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg was annexed by France, becoming the <a href="/wiki/For%C3%AAts" title="Forêts"><i>département des forêts</i></a> (department of forests), and the incorporation of the former Duchy as a <i>département</i> into France was formalised at the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Campo_Formio" title="Treaty of Campo Formio">Treaty of Campo Formio</a> in 1797.<sup id="cite_ref-Kreins_2003,_p.64_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kreins_2003,_p.64-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
From the start of the occupation the new French officials in Luxembourg, who spoke only French, implemented many republican reforms, among them the principle of <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Luxembourg#State_intervention" title="Religion in Luxembourg">laicism</a>, which led to an outcry in strongly Catholic Luxembourg. Additionally French was implemented as the only official language and Luxembourgish people were barred access to all civil services.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.65_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.65-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> When the French Army introduced military duty for the local population, riots broke out which culminated in 1798 when Luxembourgish peasants started a rebellion.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.65_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.65-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Even though the French managed to rapidly suppress this revolt called <i><a href="/wiki/Peasants%27_War_(1798)" title="Peasants&#39; War (1798)">Klëppelkrich</a></i>, it had a profound effect on the historical memory of the country and its citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, many republican ideas of this era continue to have a lasting effect on Luxembourg: one of the many examples features the implementation of the Napoleonic <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Code" title="Napoleonic Code">Code Civil</a> which was introduced in 1804 and is still valid today.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="National_awakening_and_independence_.281815.E2.80.931890.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="National_awakening_and_independence_(1815–1890)">National awakening and independence (1815–1890)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: National awakening and independence (1815–1890)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1815)" title="Treaty of Paris (1815)">defeat</a> of <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleon</a> in 1815, the Duchy of Luxembourg was restored. However, as the territory had been part of the Holy Roman Empire as well as the Habsburgian Netherlands in the past, both the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Prussia" title="Kingdom of Prussia">Kingdom of Prussia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="United Kingdom of the Netherlands">United Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> now claimed possession of the territory. At the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna" title="Congress of Vienna">Congress of Vienna</a> the great powers decided that Luxembourg would become a member state of the newly formed <a href="/wiki/German_Confederation" title="German Confederation">German Confederation</a>, but at the same time <a href="/wiki/William_I_of_the_Netherlands" title="William I of the Netherlands">William I of the Netherlands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_the_Netherlands" title="Monarchy of the Netherlands">King of the Netherlands</a>, would become, in <a href="/wiki/Personal_union" title="Personal union">personal union</a>, the head of state. To satisfy Prussia, it was decided that not only the <a href="/wiki/Fortress_of_Luxembourg" title="Fortress of Luxembourg">Fortress of Luxembourg</a> be manned by Prussian troops, but also that large parts of Luxembourgish territory (mainly the areas around Bitburg and St. Vith) become Prussian possessions.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> This marked the second time that the Duchy of Luxembourg was reduced in size, and is generally known as the <a href="/wiki/Partitions_of_Luxembourg" title="Partitions of Luxembourg">Second Partition of Luxembourg</a>. To compensate the Duchy for this loss, it was decided to elevate the Duchy to a <a href="/wiki/Grand_duchy" title="Grand duchy">Grand-Duchy</a>, thus giving the Dutch monarchs the additional title of <a href="/wiki/Grand-Duke_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand-Duke of Luxembourg">Grand-Duke of Luxembourg</a>.
</p><p>After <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a> became an independent country following the victorious <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Revolution" title="Belgian Revolution">Belgian Revolution of 1830–1831</a>, it claimed the entire Grand-Duchy of Luxembourg as being part of Belgium, however the Dutch King who was also Grand Duke of Luxembourg, as well as Prussia, didn't want to lose their grip on the mighty fortress of Luxembourg and did not agree with the Belgian claims.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The dispute would be solved at the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_London_(1839)" title="Treaty of London (1839)">1839 Treaty of London</a> where the decision of the <a href="/wiki/Partitions_of_Luxembourg" title="Partitions of Luxembourg">Third Partition of Luxembourg</a> was taken. This time the territory was reduced by more than half, as the predominantly <a href="/wiki/Francophone" class="mw-redirect" title="Francophone">francophone</a> <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_(Belgium)" title="Luxembourg (Belgium)">western part of the country</a> (but also the then Luxembourgish-speaking part of <a href="/wiki/Arelerland" title="Arelerland">Arelerland</a>) was transferred to the new state of Belgium and with it giving Luxembourg its modern-day borders. The treaty of 1839 also established full independence of the remaining Germanic-speaking Grand-Duchy of Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1842 Luxembourg joined the German Customs Union (<i><a href="/wiki/Zollverein" title="Zollverein">Zollverein</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> This resulted in the opening of the German market, the development of <a href="/wiki/Steel_industry_in_Luxembourg" title="Steel industry in Luxembourg">Luxembourg's steel industry</a>, and expansion of <a href="/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_Nationale_des_Chemins_de_Fer_Luxembourgeois" title="Société Nationale des Chemins de Fer Luxembourgeois">Luxembourg's railway network</a> from 1855 to 1875.
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Crisis" title="Luxembourg Crisis">Luxembourg Crisis</a> of 1866 nearly led to war between Prussia and France, as both were unwilling to see the other taking influence over Luxembourg and its mighty fortress, the Grand Duchy's independence and neutrality were reaffirmed by the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_London,_1867" class="mw-redirect" title="Treaty of London, 1867">Second Treaty of London</a> and Prussia was finally willing to withdraw its troops from the Fortress of Luxembourg under the condition that the fortifications would be dismantled. That happened the same year.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> At the time of the <a href="/wiki/Franco-Prussian_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Franco-Prussian war">Franco-Prussian war</a> in 1870, Luxembourg's neutrality was respected, and neither France nor Germany invaded the country.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a result of the recurring disputes between the major European powers, the people of Luxembourg gradually developed a consciousness of independence and a national awakening took place in the 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The people of Luxembourg began referring to themselves as <i><a href="/wiki/Luxembourgers" title="Luxembourgers">Luxembourgers</a></i>, rather than being part of one of the larger surrounding nations. This consciousness of <i><a href="/wiki/Mir_w%C3%ABlle_bleiwe_wat_mir_sinn" title="Mir wëlle bleiwe wat mir sinn">Mir wëlle bleiwe wat mir sinn</a></i> culminated in 1890, when the last step towards full independence was finally taken: due to a succession crisis the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_monarchy" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch monarchy">Dutch monarchy</a> ceased to hold the title Grand-Duke of Luxembourg. Beginning with <a href="/wiki/Adolphe,_Grand_Duke_of_Luxembourg" title="Adolphe, Grand Duke of Luxembourg">Adolph of Nassau-Weilburg</a>, the Grand-Duchy would have <a href="/wiki/Nassau-Weilburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Nassau-Weilburg">their own monarchy</a>, thus reaffirming its full independence.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Two_German_occupations_and_interwar_political_crisis_.281890.E2.80.931945.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Two_German_occupations_and_interwar_political_crisis_(1890–1945)">Two German occupations and interwar political crisis (1890–1945)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Two German occupations and interwar political crisis (1890–1945)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Alsace-lorraine.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Alsace-lorraine.JPG/170px-Alsace-lorraine.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="226" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Alsace-lorraine.JPG/255px-Alsace-lorraine.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Alsace-lorraine.JPG/340px-Alsace-lorraine.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1701" data-file-height="2262" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Alsace-lorraine.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Frontier with Alsace-Lorraine from 1871 to 1918</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/German_occupation_of_Luxembourg_during_World_War_I" title="German occupation of Luxembourg during World War I">German occupation of Luxembourg during World War I</a> and <a href="/wiki/German_occupation_of_Luxembourg_during_World_War_II" title="German occupation of Luxembourg during World War II">German occupation of Luxembourg during World War II</a></div>
<p>In August 1914, during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Imperial Germany">Imperial Germany</a> violated Luxembourg's <a href="/wiki/Neutral_country" title="Neutral country">neutrality</a> by invading it in order to defeat France. Nevertheless, despite the <a href="/wiki/German_occupation_of_Luxembourg_during_World_War_I" title="German occupation of Luxembourg during World War I">German occupation</a>, Luxembourg was allowed to maintain much of its independence and political mechanisms.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Unaware of the fact that Germany secretly planned to annex the Grand-Duchy in case of a German victory (the <a href="/wiki/Septemberprogramm" title="Septemberprogramm">Septemberprogramm</a>), the Luxembourgish government continued to pursue a policy of strict neutrality. However, the Luxembourgish population did not believe Germany's good intentions, fearing that Germany would annex Luxembourg. Around 3,700 Luxembourgers served in the French army, of whom 2,000 died.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Their sacrifices have been commemorated at the <a href="/wiki/G%C3%ABlle_Fra" title="Gëlle Fra">Gëlle Fra</a>.
</p><p>After the war, Grand-Duchess <a href="/wiki/Marie-Ad%C3%A9la%C3%AFde,_Grand_Duchess_of_Luxembourg" title="Marie-Adélaïde, Grand Duchess of Luxembourg">Marie-Adélaïde</a>, was seen by many people (including the French and Belgian governments) as having collaborated with the Germans and calls for her abdication and the establishment of a <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">Republic</a> became louder.<sup id="cite_ref-Thewes_2003,_p._81_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thewes_2003,_p._81-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Kreins_2003,_p._89_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kreins_2003,_p._89-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> After the retreat of the <a href="/wiki/German_Imperial_Army" class="mw-redirect" title="German Imperial Army">German army</a>, communists in Luxembourg City and <a href="/wiki/Esch-sur-Alzette" title="Esch-sur-Alzette">Esch-sur-Alzette</a> tried to establish a <a href="/wiki/Soviet_republic_(system_of_government)" title="Soviet republic (system of government)">soviet worker's republic</a> similar to the <a href="/wiki/German_Revolution_of_1918%E2%80%931919" title="German Revolution of 1918–1919">ones emerging in Germany</a>, but these attempts lasted only 2 days.<sup id="cite_ref-Kreins_2003,_p._89_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kreins_2003,_p._89-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Thewes_2003,_p._81_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thewes_2003,_p._81-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> 
In November 1918, a motion in the <a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_(Luxembourg)" title="Chamber of Deputies (Luxembourg)">Chamber of Deputies</a> demanding the <a href="/wiki/Abolition_of_monarchy" title="Abolition of monarchy">abolition of the monarchy</a> was defeated narrowly by 21 votes to 19 (with 3 abstentions).<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>France questioned the Luxembourgish government's, and especially Marie-Adélaïde's, neutrality during the war, and calls for an annexation of Luxembourg to either France or Belgium grew louder in both countries.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.85_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.85-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> 
In January 1919, a company of the <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish_Army" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourgish Army">Luxembourgish Army</a> rebelled, declaring itself to be the army of the new republic, but French troops intervened and put an end to the rebellion.<sup id="cite_ref-Michel_Pauly_p.85_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michel_Pauly_p.85-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, the disloyalty shown by her own armed forces was too much for Marie-Adélaïde, who abdicated in favor of her sister <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Charlotte" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duchess Charlotte">Charlotte</a> 5 days later.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> 
The same year, in a <a href="/wiki/1919_Luxembourg_referendum" title="1919 Luxembourg referendum">popular referendum</a>, 77.8% of the Luxembourgish population declared in favor of maintaining monarchy and rejected the establishment of a republic. 
During this time, Belgium pushed for an annexation of Luxembourg. However, all such claims were ultimately dismissed at the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Peace_Conference_(1919%E2%80%931920)" title="Paris Peace Conference (1919–1920)">Paris Peace Conference</a>, thus securing Luxembourg's independence.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1940, after the outbreak of <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, Luxembourg's neutrality was violated again when <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/Wehrmacht" title="Wehrmacht">Wehrmacht</a></i> <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Invasion of Luxembourg">entered the country</a>, "entirely without justification".<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> In contrast to the First World War, under the <a href="/wiki/German_occupation_of_Luxembourg_during_World_War_II" title="German occupation of Luxembourg during World War II">German occupation of Luxembourg during World War II</a>, the country was treated as German territory and informally annexed to the adjacent province of <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gau_Moselland" title="Gau Moselland">Gau Moselland</a>. This time, Luxembourg did not remain neutral as Luxembourg's <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_government-in-exile" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg government-in-exile">government in exile</a> based in London supported the <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II" title="Allies of World War II">Allies</a>, sending a small group of volunteers who participated in the <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Normandy" class="mw-redirect" title="Invasion of Normandy">Normandy invasion</a>, and multiple <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish_Resistance" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourgish Resistance">resistance groups</a> formed inside the occupied country.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With 2.45% of its prewar population killed, and a third of all buildings in Luxembourg being destroyed or heavily damaged (mainly due to the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Bulge" title="Battle of the Bulge">Battle of the Bulge</a>), Luxembourg suffered the highest such loss in Western Europe and its commitment to the Allied war effort was never questioned.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Around 1,000-2,500 of Luxembourg's Jews were murdered in <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust_in_Luxembourg" title="The Holocaust in Luxembourg">the Holocaust</a>.
</p>
<h3><span id="Modern_history:_Integration_into_NATO_and_European_Union_.281945.E2.80.93.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Modern_history:_Integration_into_NATO_and_European_Union_(1945–)">Modern history: Integration into NATO and European Union (1945–)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Modern history: Integration into NATO and European Union (1945–)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Grand Duchy became a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> in 1945. Luxembourg's neutral status under the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Luxembourg" title="Constitution of Luxembourg">constitution</a> formally ended in 1948, and in April 1949 it also became a founding member of <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Cold_War" title="Cold War">Cold War</a>, Luxembourg continued its involvements on the side of the <a href="/wiki/Western_Bloc" title="Western Bloc">Western Bloc</a>. In the early fifties a small contingent of troops fought in the <a href="/wiki/Korean_War" title="Korean War">Korean War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> 
Luxembourg troops have also deployed to Afghanistan, to support <a href="/wiki/ISAF" class="mw-redirect" title="ISAF">ISAF</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In the 1950's, Luxembourg became one of the six founding countries of the <a href="/wiki/European_Communities" title="European Communities">European Communities</a>, following the 1952 establishment of the <a href="/wiki/European_Coal_and_Steel_Community" title="European Coal and Steel Community">European Coal and Steel Community</a>, and subsequent 1958 creations of the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Community" title="European Economic Community">European Economic Community</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_Atomic_Energy_Community" class="mw-redirect" title="European Atomic Energy Community">European Atomic Energy Community</a>. In 1993, the former two of these were incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>. With <a href="/wiki/Robert_Schuman" title="Robert Schuman">Robert Schuman</a> (one of the founding fathers of the EU), <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Werner" title="Pierre Werner">Pierre Werner</a> (considered the father of the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">Euro</a>), <a href="/wiki/Gaston_Thorn" title="Gaston Thorn">Gaston Thorn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jacques_Santer" title="Jacques Santer">Jacques Santer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jean-Claude_Juncker" title="Jean-Claude Juncker">Jean-Claude Juncker</a> (all former Presidents of the <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission">European Commission</a>), Luxembourgish politicians contributed substantially to the EU's formation and establishment. In 1999, Luxembourg joined the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a>. Thereafter, the country was elected non-permanent member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council" title="United Nations Security Council">United Nations Security Council</a> (2013–14).
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Steel_industry" class="mw-redirect" title="Steel industry">steel industry</a> exploiting the <a href="/wiki/Red_Lands" title="Red Lands">Red Lands</a>' rich iron-ore grounds in the beginning of the 20th century drove Luxembourg's industrialization. After the decline of the steel industry in the 1970s, the country focused on establishing itself as <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Luxembourg" title="Economy of Luxembourg">a global financial center</a> and developed into the banking hub it is reputed to be. Since the beginning of the 21st century, its governments have focused on developing the country into a <a href="/wiki/Knowledge_economy" title="Knowledge economy">knowledge economy</a>, with the founding of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Luxembourg" title="University of Luxembourg">University of Luxembourg</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Space_Agency" title="Luxembourg Space Agency">national space program</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Luxembourg" title="Politics of Luxembourg">Politics of Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Luxembourg" title="Law of Luxembourg">Law of Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg/220px-Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg/330px-Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg/440px-Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4254" data-file-height="2940" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Palacio_Gran_Ducal_de_Luxemburgo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Grand_Ducal_Palace,_Luxembourg" title="Grand Ducal Palace, Luxembourg">Grand Ducal Palace</a> in <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg City</a>, the official residence of the <a href="/wiki/Grand_duke_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand duke of Luxembourg">grand duke of Luxembourg</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg/220px-Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="184" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg/330px-Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg/440px-Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3058" data-file-height="2559" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg_City_Chambre_des_d%C3%A9put%C3%A9s_Nov_2009.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Hall of the Chamber of Deputies, the meeting place of the Luxembourgish national legislature, the <a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Chamber of Deputies of Luxembourg">Chamber of Deputies</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg City</a></div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg is described as a "<a href="/wiki/Democracy_Index#Definitions" title="Democracy Index">full democracy</a>",<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> with a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_democracy" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliamentary democracy">parliamentary democracy</a> headed by a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarch" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitutional monarch">constitutional monarch</a>. Executive power is exercised by the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duke of Luxembourg">grand duke</a> and the cabinet, which consists of several other ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Luxembourg" title="Constitution of Luxembourg">Constitution of Luxembourg</a>, the supreme law of Luxembourg, was adopted on 17 October 1868.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The grand duke has the power to dissolve the <a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_(Luxembourg)" title="Chamber of Deputies (Luxembourg)">legislature</a>, in which case new elections must be held within three months. But since 1919, sovereignty has resided with the nation, exercised by the grand duke in accordance with the Constitution and the law.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Legislative power is vested in the <a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Chamber of Deputies of Luxembourg">Chamber of Deputies</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> legislature of sixty members, who are directly elected to five-year terms from four <a href="/wiki/Legislative_circonscriptions_(Luxembourg)" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative circonscriptions (Luxembourg)">constituencies</a>. A second body, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_State_of_Luxembourg" title="Council of State of Luxembourg">Council of State</a> (<i>Conseil d'État</i>), composed of 21 ordinary citizens appointed by the grand duke, advises the Chamber of Deputies in the drafting of legislation.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg has three lower tribunals (<i>justices de paix</i>; in <a href="/wiki/Esch-sur-Alzette" title="Esch-sur-Alzette">Esch-sur-Alzette</a>, the city of <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Diekirch" title="Diekirch">Diekirch</a>), two district tribunals (Luxembourg and Diekirch), and a <a href="/wiki/Superior_court_of_justice_(Luxembourg)" class="mw-redirect" title="Superior court of justice (Luxembourg)">Superior Court of Justice</a> (Luxembourg), which includes the Court of Appeal and the Court of Cassation. There is also an Administrative Tribunal and an Administrative Court, as well as a Constitutional Court, all of which are located in the capital.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Cantons_of_Luxembourg" title="Cantons of Luxembourg">Cantons of Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_Luxembourg" title="Communes of Luxembourg">Communes of Luxembourg</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Luxembourg" title="Administrative divisions of Luxembourg">Administrative divisions of Luxembourg</a></div>
<p>Luxembourg is divided into 12 <a href="/wiki/Cantons_of_Luxembourg" title="Cantons of Luxembourg">cantons</a>, which are further divided into 102 <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_Luxembourg" title="Communes of Luxembourg">communes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Twelve of the communes have <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="List of cities in Luxembourg">city status</a>; the city of <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg</a> is the largest.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tnone center"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="margin-right:20px;width:286px;max-width:286px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:373px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:LuxembourgPartitionsMap_english.png" class="image"><img alt=""
