//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/6c/LuxembourgPartitionsMap_english.png 2x" data-file-width="433" data-file-height="569" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">There have been three <a href="/wiki/Partitions_of_Luxembourg" title="Partitions of Luxembourg">partitions of Luxembourg</a> between 1659 and 1839. Together, they reduced the territory of Luxembourg from 10,700&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (4,100&#160;sq&#160;mi) to the present-day area of 2,586&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (998&#160;sq&#160;mi). The remainder forms parts of modern day <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, France, and Germany.</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:283px;max-width:283px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:373px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Groothertogdom_LuxemburgKantons.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/5a/Groothertogdom_LuxemburgKantons.png" decoding="async" width="281" height="374" data-file-width="203" data-file-height="270" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Cantons_of_Luxembourg" title="Cantons of Luxembourg">Cantons of Luxembourg</a>:<br /><a href="/wiki/Capellen_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Capellen (canton)">Capellen</a> (1) - <a href="/wiki/Clervaux_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Clervaux (canton)">Clervaux</a>(2) - <a href="/wiki/Diekirch_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Diekirch (canton)">Diekirch</a>(3) - <a href="/wiki/Echternach_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Echternach (canton)">Echternach</a>(4) - <a href="/wiki/Esch-sur-Alzette_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Esch-sur-Alzette (canton)">Esch-sur-Alzette</a>(5) - <a href="/wiki/Grevenmacher_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Grevenmacher (canton)">Grevenmacher</a>(6) - <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg (canton)">Luxembourg</a>(7) - <a href="/wiki/Mersch_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mersch (canton)">Mersch</a>(8) - <a href="/wiki/Redange_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Redange (canton)">Redange</a>(9) - <a href="/wiki/Remich_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Remich (canton)">Remich</a>(10) - <a href="/wiki/Vianden_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Vianden (canton)">Vianden</a>(11) - <a href="/wiki/Wiltz_(canton)" class="mw-redirect" title="Wiltz (canton)">Wiltz</a>(12)</div></div></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Luxembourg" title="Foreign relations of Luxembourg">Foreign relations of Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg/220px-EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="83" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg/330px-EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg/440px-EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7849" data-file-height="2945" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:EU_Court_of_Justice_overview.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Court_of_Justice_of_the_European_Union" title="Court of Justice of the European Union">Court of Justice of the European Union</a>, seated in <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg City</a></div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg has long been a prominent supporter of European political and <a href="/wiki/Economic_integration" title="Economic integration">economic integration</a>. In 1921, Luxembourg and Belgium formed the <a href="/wiki/Belgium%E2%80%93Luxembourg_Economic_Union" title="Belgium–Luxembourg Economic Union">Belgium–Luxembourg Economic Union</a> (BLEU) to create a regime of inter-exchangeable currency and a common <a href="/wiki/Customs_(tax)" class="mw-redirect" title="Customs (tax)">customs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Benelux_Economic_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Benelux Economic Union">Benelux Economic Union</a> and was one of the founding members of the European Economic Community (now the European Union). It also participates in the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Agreement" title="Schengen Agreement">Schengen Group</a> (named after <a href="/wiki/Schengen,_Luxembourg" title="Schengen, Luxembourg">the Luxembourg village of Schengen</a> where the agreements were signed).<sup id="cite_ref-:1_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> At the same time, the majority of Luxembourgers have consistently believed that European unity makes sense only in the context of a dynamic transatlantic relationship, and thus have traditionally pursued a pro-<a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, pro-US foreign policy. <sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg is the site of the <a href="/wiki/Court_of_Justice_of_the_European_Union" title="Court of Justice of the European Union">Court of Justice of the European Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/European_Court_of_Auditors" title="European Court of Auditors">European Court of Auditors</a>, the Statistical Office of the European Union (<a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a>) and other vital EU organs. The <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_the_European_Parliament" title="Secretariat of the European Parliament">Secretariat of the European Parliament</a> is located in Luxembourg, but the Parliament usually meets in <a href="/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels">Brussels</a> and sometimes in <a href="/wiki/Strasbourg" title="Strasbourg">Strasbourg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg is also site of the <a href="/wiki/EFTA_Court" title="EFTA Court">EFTA Court</a>, which is responsible for the three <a href="/wiki/EFTA" class="mw-redirect" title="EFTA">EFTA</a> members who are part of the European Single Market through the <a href="/wiki/EEA_Agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="EEA Agreement">EEA Agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Armed_Forces" title="Luxembourg Armed Forces">Luxembourg Armed Forces</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg/220px-Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg/330px-Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg/440px-Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Soldats_luxembourgeois.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Luxembourgish soldiers on parade during National Day, <i>Grand Duke Day</i>, 23 June</div></div></div>
<p>The Luxembourgish army is mostly based in its casern, the <i>Centre militaire Caserne Grand-Duc Jean</i> on the <i>Härebierg</i> in Diekirch. The general staff is based in the capital, the <i>État-Major</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The army is under <a href="/wiki/Civilian_control_of_the_military" title="Civilian control of the military">civilian control</a>, with the grand duke as <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-Chief" class="mw-redirect" title="Commander-in-Chief">Commander-in-Chief</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Defence_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Defence of Luxembourg">Minister for Defense</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Bausch" title="François Bausch">François Bausch</a>, oversees army operations. The professional head of the army is the <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_Defence_(Luxembourg)" title="Chief of Defence (Luxembourg)">Chief of Defense</a>, who answers to the minister and holds the rank of general.
</p><p>Being landlocked, Luxembourg has no navy. Seventeen NATO <a href="/wiki/Airborne_Warning_And_Control_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Airborne Warning And Control System">AWACS</a> airplanes are registered as aircraft of Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-Aeroflight_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Aeroflight-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> In accordance with a joint agreement with Belgium, both countries have put forth funding for one <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A400M" class="mw-redirect" title="Airbus A400M">A400M</a> military cargo plane.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg has participated in the <a href="/wiki/Eurocorps" title="Eurocorps">Eurocorps</a>, has contributed troops to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Protection_Force" title="United Nations Protection Force">UNPROFOR</a> and <a href="/wiki/IFOR" class="mw-redirect" title="IFOR">IFOR</a> missions in former <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" title="Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a>, and has participated with a small contingent in the <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> <a href="/wiki/SFOR" class="mw-redirect" title="SFOR">SFOR</a> mission in <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a>. Luxembourg troops have also deployed to <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>, to support <a href="/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force" title="International Security Assistance Force">ISAF</a>. The army has also participated in humanitarian relief missions such as setting up refugee camps for <a href="/wiki/Kurdish_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Kurdish people">Kurds</a> and providing emergency supplies to Albania.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Luxembourg" title="Geography of Luxembourg">Geography of Luxembourg</a></div>
<p>Luxembourg is one of Europe's smallest countries, ranking <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_area" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by area">167th</a> in size of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states" title="List of sovereign states">194 independent countries of the world</a>; it is about 2,586 square kilometers (998&#160;sq&#160;mi) in size, and measures 82&#160;km (51&#160;mi) long and 57&#160;km (35&#160;mi) wide. It lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/49th_parallel_north" title="49th parallel north">49°</a> and <a href="/wiki/51st_parallel_north" title="51st parallel north">51° N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/5th_meridian_east" title="5th meridian east">5°</a> and <a href="/wiki/7th_meridian_east" title="7th meridian east">7° E</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg2022OSM.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Luxembourg2022OSM.png/220px-Luxembourg2022OSM.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="319" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Luxembourg2022OSM.png/330px-Luxembourg2022OSM.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Luxembourg2022OSM.png/440px-Luxembourg2022OSM.png 2x" data-file-width="3025" data-file-height="4393" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg2022OSM.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The largest towns are <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg (city)">Luxembourg</a>, <a href="/wiki/Esch-sur-Alzette" title="Esch-sur-Alzette">Esch-sur-Alzette</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dudelange" title="Dudelange">Dudelange</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Differdange" title="Differdange">Differdange</a>.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG/220px-Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG/330px-Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG/440px-Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3733" data-file-height="2493" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vue_op_Alschent-001.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Typical Luxembourg countryside near <a href="/wiki/Alscheid" title="Alscheid">Alscheid</a></div></div></div>
<p>To the east, Luxembourg borders the German <i><a href="/wiki/States_of_Germany" title="States of Germany">Bundesländer</a></i> of <a href="/wiki/Rhineland-Palatinate" title="Rhineland-Palatinate">Rhineland-Palatinate</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saarland" title="Saarland">Saarland</a>, and to the south, it borders the French <i><a href="/wiki/Regions_of_France" title="Regions of France">région</a></i> of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Est" title="Grand Est">Grand Est</a> (<a href="/wiki/Lorraine" title="Lorraine">Lorraine</a>). The Grand Duchy borders Belgium's <a href="/wiki/Wallonia" title="Wallonia">Wallonia</a>, in particular the Belgian <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Belgium" title="Provinces of Belgium">provinces</a> of <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_(Belgium)" title="Luxembourg (Belgium)">Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/Li%C3%A8ge_Province" title="Liège Province">Liège</a>, part of which comprises the <a href="/wiki/German-speaking_Community_of_Belgium" title="German-speaking Community of Belgium">German-speaking Community of Belgium</a>, to the west and to the north, respectively.
</p><p>The northern third of the country is known as the <a href="/wiki/Oesling" title="Oesling">Oesling</a>, and forms part of the <a href="/wiki/Ardennes" title="Ardennes">Ardennes</a>. It is dominated by hills and low mountains, including the <a href="/wiki/Kneiff" title="Kneiff">Kneiff</a> near <a href="/wiki/Wilwerdange" title="Wilwerdange">Wilwerdange</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> which is the highest point, at 560 meters (1,840&#160;ft). Other mountains are the <a href="/wiki/Buurgplaatz" title="Buurgplaatz">Buurgplaatz</a> at 559 meters (1,834&#160;ft) near <a href="/wiki/Huldange" title="Huldange">Huldange</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Napol%C3%A9onsgaard" title="Napoléonsgaard">Napoléonsgaard</a> at 554 meters (1,818&#160;ft) near <a href="/wiki/Rambrouch" title="Rambrouch">Rambrouch</a>. The region is sparsely populated, with only one town (<a href="/wiki/Wiltz" title="Wiltz">Wiltz</a>) with a population of more than four thousand people.
</p><p>The southern two-thirds of the country is called the <a href="/wiki/Gutland_(Luxembourg)" title="Gutland (Luxembourg)">Gutland</a>, and is more densely populated than the Oesling. It is also more diverse and can be divided into five geographic sub-regions. The <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_plateau" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg plateau">Luxembourg plateau</a>, in south-central Luxembourg, is a large, flat, <a href="/wiki/Sandstone" title="Sandstone">sandstone</a> formation, and the site of the city of Luxembourg. <a href="/wiki/Little_Switzerland_(Luxembourg)" title="Little Switzerland (Luxembourg)">Little Switzerland</a>, in the east of Luxembourg, has craggy terrain and thick forests. The <a href="/wiki/Moselle_(river)" class="mw-redirect" title="Moselle (river)">Moselle</a> valley is the lowest-lying region, running along the southeastern border. The <a href="/wiki/Red_Lands" title="Red Lands">Red Lands</a>, in the far south and southwest, are Luxembourg's industrial heartland and home to many of Luxembourg's largest towns.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Grund,_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg/220px-Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg/330px-Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg/440px-Grund%2C_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Grund,_Luxembourg_from_wall_above.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View of the <a href="/wiki/Grund,_Luxembourg" title="Grund, Luxembourg">Grund</a> along <a href="/wiki/Alzette" title="Alzette">Alzette</a> river in the historical heart of Luxembourg City</div></div></div>
<p>The border between Luxembourg and Germany is formed by three rivers: the <a href="/wiki/Moselle" title="Moselle">Moselle</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sauer" title="Sauer">Sauer</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Our_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Our River">Our</a>. Other major rivers are the <a href="/wiki/Alzette" title="Alzette">Alzette</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Attert_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Attert River">Attert</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Clerve" title="Clerve">Clerve</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Wiltz_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Wiltz River">Wiltz</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Valley" title="Valley">valleys</a> of the mid-Sauer and Attert form the border between the Gutland and the Oesling.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>According to the 2012 <a href="/wiki/Environmental_Performance_Index" title="Environmental Performance Index">Environmental Performance Index</a>, Luxembourg is one of the world's best performers in environmental protection, ranking 4th out of 132 assessed countries.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> In 2020, it ranked second out of 180 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg also ranks 6th among the top ten most livable cities in the world by Mercer's.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> The country wants to cut <a href="/wiki/GHG_emissions" class="mw-redirect" title="GHG emissions">GHG emissions</a> by 55% in 10 years and reach zero emissions by 2050. Luxembourg wants to increase its organic farming fivefold.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 1.12/10, ranking it 164th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Luxembourg has an <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_climate" title="Oceanic climate">oceanic climate</a> (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen</a>: <i>Cfb</i>), marked by high precipitation, particularly in late summer. The summers are warm and winters cool.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Luxembourg" title="Economy of Luxembourg">Economy of Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/170px-BlueEurozone.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="182" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/255px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/340px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="645" data-file-height="690" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Luxembourg is part of the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a>, the EU single market, and the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a> (dark blue).</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Luxembourg_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luxembourg_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Graphical depiction of Luxembourg's product exports in 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg's stable and high-income <a href="/wiki/Economic_system" title="Economic system">market economy</a> features moderate growth, low inflation, and a high level of innovation.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Unemployment is traditionally low, though it reached 6.1% by May 2012, due largely to the <a href="/wiki/2008_global_financial_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 global financial crisis">2008 global financial crisis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, according to the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">IMF</a>, Luxembourg was the world's second-richest country, with a per capita GDP on a purchasing-power parity (PPP) basis of $80,119.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Its GDP per capita in purchasing power standards was 261% of the EU average (100%) in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg ranks 13th in <a href="/wiki/The_Heritage_Foundation" title="The Heritage Foundation">The Heritage Foundation</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Index_of_Economic_Freedom" title="Index of Economic Freedom">Index of Economic Freedom</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> 26th in the United Nations <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a>, and 4th in the Economist Intelligence Unit's <a href="/wiki/Quality-of-life_index" class="mw-redirect" title="Quality-of-life index">quality of life index</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> It ranked 19th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The industrial sector, dominated by steel until the 1960s, has since diversified to include chemicals, rubber, and other products. During recent decades, growth in the financial sector has more than compensated for the decline in <a href="/wiki/Steel_industry_in_Luxembourg" title="Steel industry in Luxembourg">steel production</a>. Services, especially banking and <a href="/wiki/Financial_export" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial export">finance</a>, account for the majority of the economic output. Luxembourg is the world's second largest investment fund center (after the United States), the most important private banking center in the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a> and Europe's leading center for reinsurance companies. Moreover, Luxembourg's government has aimed to attract Internet startups, with <a href="/wiki/Skype" title="Skype">Skype</a> and <a href="/wiki/Amazon.com" class="mw-redirect" title="Amazon.com">Amazon</a> being two of the many Internet companies that have shifted their regional headquarters to Luxembourg. Other high-tech companies have established themselves in Luxembourg, including <a href="/wiki/3d_scanner" class="mw-redirect" title="3d scanner">3D scanner</a> developer/manufacturer <a href="/wiki/Artec_3D" title="Artec 3D">Artec 3D</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In April 2009, concern about Luxembourg's banking secrecy laws, as well as its reputation as a <a href="/wiki/Tax_haven" title="Tax haven">tax haven</a>, led to its being added to a "gray list" of nations with questionable banking arrangements by the <a href="/wiki/G20" title="G20">G20</a>. In response, the country soon adopted OECD standards on exchange of information and was subsequently added into the category of "jurisdictions that have substantially implemented the internationally agreed tax standard".<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> In March 2010, the <i>Sunday Telegraph</i> reported that most of <a href="/wiki/Kim_Jong-il" title="Kim Jong-il">Kim Jong-Il</a>'s $4 billion in secret accounts was in Luxembourg banks.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Amazon.co.uk also benefits from Luxembourg tax loopholes by channeling substantial U.K. revenues, as reported by <i>The Guardian</i> in April 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg ranked third on the <a href="/wiki/Tax_Justice_Network" title="Tax Justice Network">Tax Justice Network</a>'s 2011 <a href="/wiki/Financial_Secrecy_Index" title="Financial Secrecy Index">Financial Secrecy Index</a> of the world's major tax havens, scoring only slightly behind the <a href="/wiki/Cayman_Islands" title="Cayman Islands">Cayman Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, Luxembourg was ranked the 2nd safest tax haven in the world, behind <a href="/wiki/Switzerland" title="Switzerland">Switzerland</a>.
</p><p>In early November 2014, just days after becoming head of the <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission">European Commission</a>, Luxembourg's former Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Jean-Claude_Juncker" title="Jean-Claude Juncker">Jean-Claude Juncker</a> was hit by media disclosures—derived from a document leak known as <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Leaks" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg Leaks">Luxembourg Leaks</a>—that Luxembourg had turned into a major European center of corporate <a href="/wiki/Tax_avoidance" title="Tax avoidance">tax avoidance</a> under his premiership.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Agriculture employed about 2.1% of Luxembourg's active population in 2010, when there were 2200 agricultural holdings with an average area per holding of 60 hectares.<sup id="cite_ref-agcensus_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-agcensus-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg has especially close trade and financial ties to Belgium and the Netherlands (see <a href="/wiki/Benelux" title="Benelux">Benelux</a>), and as a member of the EU it enjoys the advantages of the open European <a href="/wiki/Market_(economics)" title="Market (economics)">market</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With $171 billion in May 2015, the country ranked 11th in the world in holdings of <a href="/wiki/U.S._Treasury_security" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Treasury security">U.S. Treasury securities</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> However, securities owned by non-Luxembourg residents, but held in custodial accounts in Luxembourg, are included in this figure.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Luxembourg's public debt totaled $15,687,000,000, or $25,554 per capita. The debt to GDP was 22.10%.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Luxembourg labor market represents 445,000 jobs occupied by 120,000 Luxembourgers, 120,000 foreign residents and 205,000 cross-border commuters. The latter pay their taxes in Luxembourg, but their education and social rights are the responsibility of their country of residence. The same applies to pensioners. Luxembourg's government has never shared its tax revenues with the local authorities on the <a href="/wiki/France%E2%80%93Luxembourg_border" title="France–Luxembourg border">French border</a>. This system is seen as one of the keys to Luxembourg's economic growth, but at the expense of the border countries.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Luxembourg" title="Transport in Luxembourg">Transport in Luxembourg</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/History_of_rail_transport_in_Luxembourg" title="History of rail transport in Luxembourg">History of rail transport in Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg/220px-LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg/330px-LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg/440px-LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:LUX_Check-In_Schalter.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Luxembourg's international airline <a href="/wiki/Luxair" title="Luxair">Luxair</a> is based at <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Airport" title="Luxembourg Airport">Luxembourg Airport</a>, the country's only international airport.</div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg has road, rail and air transport facilities and services. The road network has been significantly modernized in recent years with 165&#160;km (103&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> of motorways connecting the capital to adjacent countries. The advent of the high-speed <a href="/wiki/TGV" title="TGV">TGV</a> link to Paris has led to renovation of the city's <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_railway_station" title="Luxembourg railway station">railway station</a> and a new passenger terminal at <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_Airport" title="Luxembourg Airport">Luxembourg Airport</a> was opened in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg city reintroduced <a href="/wiki/Trams_in_Luxembourg" title="Trams in Luxembourg">trams</a> in December 2017 and there are plans to open <a href="/wiki/Light-rail" class="mw-redirect" title="Light-rail">light-rail</a> lines in adjacent areas within the next few years.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are 681 cars per 1000 persons in Luxembourg—higher than most of other <a href="/wiki/State_(polity)" title="State (polity)">states</a>, and surpassed by the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a>, and other small states like <a href="/wiki/Monaco" title="Monaco">Principality of Monaco</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Marino" title="San Marino">San Marino</a>, <a href="/wiki/Liechtenstein" title="Liechtenstein">Liechtenstein</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Gibraltar" title="Gibraltar">British overseas territory of Gibraltar</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Brunei" title="Brunei">Brunei</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 29 February 2020, Luxembourg became the first country to introduce no-charge public transportation, which will be almost completely funded by public expenditure.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Luxembourg" title="Telecommunications in Luxembourg">Telecommunications in Luxembourg</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_industry" title="Telecommunications industry">telecommunications industry</a> in Luxembourg is liberalized and the electronic communications networks are significantly developed. Competition between the different operators is guaranteed by the legislative framework Paquet Telecom<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> of the Government of 2011 which transposes the European Telecom Directives into Luxembourgish law. This encourages the investment in networks and services. The regulator ILR – Institut Luxembourgeois de Régulation<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> ensures the compliance to these legal rules.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg has modern and widely deployed optical fiber and cable networks throughout the country. In 2010, the Luxembourg Government launched its National strategy for very high-speed networks with the aim to become a global leader in terms of very high-speed broadband by achieving full 1 Gbit/s coverage of the country by 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, Luxembourg had an <a href="/wiki/Next-generation_access" class="mw-redirect" title="Next-generation access">NGA</a> coverage of 75%.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> In April 2013 Luxembourg featured the 6th highest download speed worldwide and the 2nd highest in Europe: 32,46 Mbit/s.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The country's location in Central Europe, stable economy and low taxes favour the telecommunication industry.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It ranks 2nd in the world in the development of the Information and Communication Technologies in the ITU ICT Development Index and 8th in the Global Broadband Quality Study 2009 by the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Oxford" title="University of Oxford">University of Oxford</a> and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Oviedo" title="University of Oviedo">University of Oviedo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg/220px-Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="162" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg/330px-Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg/440px-Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3782" data-file-height="2790" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Centre_Drosbach_Luxembourg_Signs_-_June_2012.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Signs in front of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Centre_Drosbach&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Centre Drosbach (page does not exist)">Centre Drosbach</a> on the Cloche d'or, in the city of <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg</a></div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg is connected to all major European Internet Exchanges (AMS-IX Amsterdam,<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> DE-CIX Frankfurt,<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> LINX London),<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> datacenters and POPs through redundant optical networks.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the country is connected to the virtual meetme room services (vmmr)<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> of the international data hub operator Ancotel.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> This enables Luxembourg to interconnect with all major telecommunication operators<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> and data carriers worldwide. The interconnection points are in Frankfurt, London, New York and Hong Kong.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Luxembourg has established itself as one of the leading <a href="/wiki/Financial_technology" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial technology">financial technology</a> (FinTech) hubs in Europe, with the Luxembourg government supporting initiatives like the Luxembourg House of Financial Technology.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some 20 data centers<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> are operating in Luxembourg. Six data centers are Tier IV Design certified: three of ebrc,<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> two of LuxConnect<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> and one of European Data Hub.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> In a survey on nine international data centers carried out in December 2012 and January 2013 and measuring availability (up-time) and performance (delay by which the data from the requested website was received), the top three positions were held by Luxembourg data centers.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Luxembourg" title="Demographics of Luxembourg">Demographics of Luxembourg</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Largest_towns">Largest towns</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Largest towns">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
