<p>The people of Luxembourg are called <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgers" title="Luxembourgers">Luxembourgers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> The immigrant population increased in the 20th century due to the arrival of immigrants from <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, France, Italy, Germany, and <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>; the latter comprised the largest group.  In 2013 about 88,000 Luxembourg inhabitants possessed <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Luxembourger" title="Portuguese Luxembourger">Portuguese</a> nationality.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, there were 537,039 permanent residents, 44.5% of which were of foreign background or foreign nationals; the largest foreign ethnic groups were the Portuguese, comprising 16.4% of the total population, followed by the French (6.6%), Italians (3.4%), Belgians (3.3%) and Germans (2.3%). Another 6.4% were of other EU background, while the remaining 6.1% were of other non-EU, but largely other European, background.<sup id="cite_ref-statnews13_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statnews13-177">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the beginning of the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_wars" class="mw-redirect" title="Yugoslav wars">Yugoslav wars</a>, Luxembourg has seen many immigrants from <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Montenegro" title="Montenegro">Montenegro</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>. Annually, over 10,000 new immigrants arrive in Luxembourg, mostly from the EU states, as well as Eastern Europe. In 2000 there were 162,000 immigrants in Luxembourg, accounting for 37% of the total population. There were an estimated 5,000 illegal immigrants in Luxembourg in 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Luxembourg" title="Languages of Luxembourg">Languages of Luxembourg</a>, <a href="/wiki/Multilingualism_in_Luxembourg" title="Multilingualism in Luxembourg">Multilingualism in Luxembourg</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_literature" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourg literature">Luxembourg literature</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:LUX-5f2.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/LUX-5f2.jpg/220px-LUX-5f2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="111" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/LUX-5f2.jpg/330px-LUX-5f2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/7e/LUX-5f2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="407" data-file-height="206" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:LUX-5f2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Coin of the former <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish_franc" class="mw-redirect" title="Luxembourgish franc">Luxembourg franc</a> in two of the country's three languages: French (obverse, left) and Luxembourgish (reverse, right)</div></div></div>
<p>As determined by law since 1984, Luxembourg has only one <a href="/wiki/National_language" title="National language">national language</a>, <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish" title="Luxembourgish">Luxembourgish</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> It is considered the mother tongue or "language of the heart" for Luxembourgers and the language they generally use to speak or write to each other. Luxembourgish is considered a <a href="/wiki/Franconian_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Franconian languages">Franconian</a> language specific to the local population, which is partially mutually intelligible with the neighboring <a href="/wiki/High_German" class="mw-redirect" title="High German">High German</a>, but which also includes more than 5,000 words of French origin.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Knowledge of Luxembourgish is a criterion for <a href="/wiki/Naturalization" title="Naturalization">naturalisation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition to Luxembourgish, <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> and <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a> are used in administrative and judicial matters, making all three <a href="/wiki/Administrative_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Administrative language">administrative languages</a> of Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Per article 4 of the law promulgated in 1984, if a citizen asks a question in Luxembourgish, German or French, the administration must reply, as far as possible, in the language in which the question was asked.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg is largely multilingual: as of 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 52% of citizens claimed Luxembourgish as their native language, 16.4% <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>, 16% French, 2% German and 13.6% different languages (mostly <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a> or <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> Even though French was the mother tongue of only 16% of residents in Luxembourg (placing 3rd), 98% of its citizens were able to speak it to a high level.<sup id="cite_ref-luxembourg.public.lu_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-luxembourg.public.lu-184">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> The vast majority of Luxembourg residents are able to speak it as a second or third language.<sup id="cite_ref-admin_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-admin-185">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> As of 2018<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, much of the population was able to speak multiple other languages: 80% of citizens reported being able to hold a conversation in English, 78% in German and 77% in Luxembourgish, claiming these languages as their respective second, third or fourth language.<sup id="cite_ref-luxembourg.public.lu_184-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-luxembourg.public.lu-184">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Each of the three official languages is used as a primary language in certain spheres of everyday life, without being exclusive. Luxembourgish is the language that Luxembourgers generally use to speak and write to each other, and there has been a recent increase in the production of novels and movies in the language; at the same time, the numerous expatriate workers (approximately 44% of the population) generally do not use it to speak to each other.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most official business and written communication is carried out in French, which is also the language mostly used for public communication, with written official statements, advertising displays and road signs generally in French. Due to the historical influence of the Napoleonic Code on the legal system of the Grand Duchy, French is also the sole language of the legislation and generally the preferred language of the government, administration and justice. Parliamentary debates are mostly conducted in Luxembourgish, whereas written government communications and official documents (e.g. administrative or judicial decisions, passports, etc.) are drafted mostly in French and sometimes additionally in German.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Although professional life is largely multilingual, French is described by private sector business leaders as the main working language of their companies (56%), followed by Luxembourgish (20%), English (18%), and German (6%).<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>German is very often used in much of the media along with French and is considered by most Luxembourgers their second language. This is mostly due to the high similarity of German to Luxembourgish but also because it is the first language taught to children in primary school (language of literacy acquisition).<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to the large community of Portuguese origin, the Portuguese language is fairly prevalent in Luxembourg, though it remains limited to the relationships inside this community. Portuguese has no official status, but the administration sometimes makes certain informative documents available in Portuguese.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Even though Luxembourg is largely multilingual today, some people claim that Luxembourg is subject of intense <a href="/wiki/Francization" title="Francization">francization</a> and that Luxembourgish and German are in danger of disappearing in the country, making Luxembourg either a unilingual Francophone country, or at best a bilingual French- and English-speaking country sometime in the far future.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-admin_185-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-admin-185">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish#Endangered_status_claims" title="Luxembourgish">Luxembourgish §&#160;Endangered status claims</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Luxembourg" title="Religion in Luxembourg">Religion in Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG/170px-Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="256" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG/255px-Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG/340px-Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="3008" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Notre_Dame_Cathedral_Luxembourg.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Notre-Dame_Cathedral,_Luxembourg" title="Notre-Dame Cathedral, Luxembourg">Notre-Dame Cathedral</a>, Luxembourg City</div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg is a <a href="/wiki/Secular_state" title="Secular state">secular state</a>, but the state recognizes certain religions as officially mandated religions. This gives the state a hand in religious administration and appointment of clergy, in exchange for which the state pays certain running costs and wages. Religions covered by such arrangements are <a href="/wiki/Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholicism">Catholicism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Judaism" title="Judaism">Judaism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greek_Orthodoxy" class="mw-redirect" title="Greek Orthodoxy">Greek Orthodoxy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anglicanism" title="Anglicanism">Anglicanism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodoxy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheranism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Calvinism" title="Calvinism">Calvinism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mennonitism" class="mw-redirect" title="Mennonitism">Mennonitism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1980, it has been illegal for the government to collect statistics on religious beliefs or practices.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> A 2000 estimate by the <a href="/wiki/The_World_Factbook" title="The World Factbook">CIA Factbook</a> is that 87% of Luxembourgers are Catholic, including the grand ducal family, with the remaining 13% being Protestants, Orthodox Christians, Jews, Muslims, and those of other or no religion.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-193">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> According to a 2010 <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Research Center</a> study, 70.4% are Christian, 2.3% Muslim, 26.8% unaffiliated, and 0.5% other religions.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to a 2005 <a href="/wiki/Eurobarometer" title="Eurobarometer">Eurobarometer</a> poll,<sup id="cite_ref-EUROBAROMETER_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EUROBAROMETER-195">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> 44% of Luxembourg citizens responded that "they believe there is a God", whereas 28% answered that "they believe there is some sort of spirit or life force", and 22% that "they do not believe there is any sort of spirit, god, or life force".
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg/220px-University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg/330px-University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg/440px-University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="683" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:University_luxemburg_lmp_main.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Luxembourg" title="University of Luxembourg">University of Luxembourg</a> is the only university based in the country.</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_secondary_schools_in_Luxembourg" title="List of secondary schools in Luxembourg">List of secondary schools in Luxembourg</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Education_in_Luxembourg" title="Education in Luxembourg">Luxembourg's education system</a> is trilingual: the first years of primary school are in Luxembourgish, before changing to German; while in secondary school, the language of instruction changes to French.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Proficiency in all three languages is required for graduation from secondary school, but half the students leave school without a certified qualification, with the children of immigrants being particularly disadvantaged.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> In addition to the three national languages, English is taught in compulsory schooling and much of the population of Luxembourg can speak English. The past two decades have highlighted the growing importance of English in several sectors, in particular the financial sector. Portuguese, the language of the largest immigrant community, is also spoken by large segments of the population, but by relatively few from outside the Portuguese-speaking community.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Luxembourg" title="University of Luxembourg">University of Luxembourg</a> is the only university based in Luxembourg. In 2014, <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_School_of_Business" title="Luxembourg School of Business">Luxembourg School of Business</a>, a graduate business school, was created through private initiative and received the accreditation from the Ministry of Higher Education and Research of Luxembourg in 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> Two American universities maintain satellite campuses in the country: <a href="/wiki/Miami_University" title="Miami University">Miami University</a> (<a href="/wiki/Miami_University_Dolibois_European_Center" title="Miami University Dolibois European Center">Dolibois European Center</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Sacred_Heart_University" title="Sacred Heart University">Sacred Heart University</a> (<a href="/wiki/Sacred_Heart_University_Luxembourg" title="Sacred Heart University Luxembourg">Luxembourg Campus</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Luxembourg" title="Health in Luxembourg">Health in Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Luxembourg" title="Healthcare in Luxembourg">Healthcare in Luxembourg</a></div>
<p>According to data from the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a>, healthcare spending on behalf of the government of Luxembourg topped $4.1 Billion, amounting to about $8,182 for each citizen in the nation.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The nation of Luxembourg collectively spent nearly 7% of its <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">Gross Domestic Product</a> on health, placing it among the highest spending countries on health services and related programs in 2010 among other well-off nations in Europe with high average income among its population.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Luxembourg" title="Culture of Luxembourg">Culture of Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/National_symbols_of_Luxembourg" title="National symbols of Luxembourg">National symbols of Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:142px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Edward_Steichen.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Edward_Steichen.jpg/140px-Edward_Steichen.jpg" decoding="async" width="140" height="264" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Edward_Steichen.jpg/210px-Edward_Steichen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Edward_Steichen.jpg/280px-Edward_Steichen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="698" data-file-height="1315" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Edward_Steichen.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Edward_Steichen" title="Edward Steichen">Edward Steichen</a>, photographer and painter</div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg has been heavily influenced by the culture of its neighbors. It retains a number of folk traditions, having been for much of its history a profoundly rural country. There are several notable museums, located mostly in the capital. These include the <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_History_and_Art" title="National Museum of History and Art">National Museum of History and Art</a> (NMHA), the <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City_History_Museum" title="Luxembourg City History Museum">Luxembourg City History Museum</a>, and the new <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Jean_Museum_of_Modern_Art" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duke Jean Museum of Modern Art">Grand Duke Jean Museum of Modern Art</a> (Mudam). The <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_Military_History_(Luxembourg)" title="National Museum of Military History (Luxembourg)">National Museum of Military History (MNHM)</a> in Diekirch is especially known for its representations of the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Bulge" title="Battle of the Bulge">Battle of the Bulge</a>. The city of Luxembourg itself is on the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_List" class="mw-redirect" title="World Heritage List">World Heritage List</a>, on account of the historical importance of its fortifications.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources" title="Wikipedia:Reliable sources"><span title="The material near this tag may rely on an unreliable source. (December 2020)">unreliable source?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The country has produced some internationally known artists, including the painters <a href="/wiki/Th%C3%A9o_Kerg" title="Théo Kerg">Théo Kerg</a>, <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Kutter" title="Joseph Kutter">Joseph Kutter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Michel_Majerus" title="Michel Majerus">Michel Majerus</a>, and photographer <a href="/wiki/Edward_Steichen" title="Edward Steichen">Edward Steichen</a>, whose <i><a href="/wiki/The_Family_of_Man" title="The Family of Man">The Family of Man</a></i> exhibition has been placed on UNESCO's <a href="/wiki/Memory_of_the_World_Programme" title="Memory of the World Programme">Memory of the World</a> register, and is now permanently housed in <a href="/wiki/Clervaux" title="Clervaux">Clervaux</a>. Editor and author <a href="/wiki/Hugo_Gernsback" title="Hugo Gernsback">Hugo Gernsback</a>, whose publications crystallized the concept of <a href="/wiki/Science_fiction" title="Science fiction">science fiction</a>, was born in Luxembourg City. Movie star <a href="/wiki/Loretta_Young" title="Loretta Young">Loretta Young</a> was of Luxembourgish descent.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg was a founding participant of the <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Luxembourg in the Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a>, and participated every year between <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1956" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1956">1956</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1993" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1993">1993</a>, with the exception of 1959. It won the competition a total of five times, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1961" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1961">1961</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1965" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1965">1965</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1972" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1972">1972</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1973" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1973">1973</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1983" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1983">1983</a> and hosted the contest in <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1962" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1962">1962</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1966" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1966">1966</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1973" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1973">1973</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1984" title="Eurovision Song Contest 1984">1984</a>, but only nine of its 38 entries were performed by <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgers" title="Luxembourgers">Luxembourgish</a> artists.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg was the first city to be named <a href="/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture" title="European Capital of Culture">European Capital of Culture</a> twice. The first time was in 1995. In 2007, the European Capital of Culture<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> was to be a cross-border area consisting of the Grand Duchy of Luxembourg, the Rheinland-Pfalz and Saarland in Germany, the Walloon Region and the German-speaking part of Belgium, and the <a href="/wiki/Lorraine" title="Lorraine">Lorraine</a> area in France. The event was an attempt to promote mobility and the exchange of ideas, crossing borders physically, psychologically, artistically and emotionally.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg was represented at the World <a href="/wiki/Expo_2010" title="Expo 2010">Expo 2010</a> in Shanghai, China, from 1 May to 31 October 2010 with its own pavilion.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> The pavilion was based on the transliteration of the word Luxembourg into Chinese, "Lu Sen Bao", which means "Forest and Fortress". It represented Luxembourg as the "Green Heart in Europe".<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Luxembourg" title="Sport in Luxembourg">Sport in Luxembourg</a></div> 
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Luxembourgish_Sportspeople_of_the_Year" title="Luxembourgish Sportspeople of the Year">Luxembourgish Sportspeople of the Year</a></div> 
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Charly_Gaul_1959_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="246" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Charly_Gaul_1959_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="352" data-file-height="510" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Charly_Gaul_1959_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Charly Gaul won three <a href="/wiki/Grand_Tour_(cycling)" title="Grand Tour (cycling)">Grand Tours</a> in his cycling career.</div></div></div>
<p>Unlike most countries in Europe, sports in Luxembourg are not concentrated upon a particular <a href="/wiki/National_sport" title="National sport">national sport</a>, but instead encompass a number of sports, both team and individual. Despite the lack of a central sporting focus, over 100,000 people in Luxembourg, out of a total population of near 500,000–600,000, are licensed members of one sports federation or another.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Stade_de_Luxembourg" title="Stade de Luxembourg">Stade de Luxembourg</a>, situated in <a href="/wiki/Gasperich" title="Gasperich">Gasperich</a>, southern <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg City</a>, is the country's <a href="/wiki/National_stadium" class="mw-redirect" title="National stadium">national stadium</a> and largest sports venue in the country with a capacity of 9,386 for sporting events, including football and rugby union, and 15,000 for concerts.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> The largest <a href="/wiki/Indoor_arena" class="mw-redirect" title="Indoor arena">indoor venue</a> in the country is <a href="/wiki/D%27Coque" title="D&#39;Coque">d'Coque</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kirchberg,_Luxembourg" title="Kirchberg, Luxembourg">Kirchberg</a>, north-eastern <a href="/wiki/Luxembourg_City" title="Luxembourg City">Luxembourg City</a>, which has a capacity of 8,300. The arena is used for <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Team_handball" class="mw-redirect" title="Team handball">handball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gymnastics" title="Gymnastics">gymnastics</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, including the final of the <a href="/wiki/2007_Women%27s_European_Volleyball_Championship" title="2007 Women&#39;s European Volleyball Championship">2007 Women's European Volleyball Championship</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Luxembourg" title="Cuisine of Luxembourg">Cuisine of Luxembourg</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG/170px-JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="256" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG/255px-JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG/340px-JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="3008" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:JuddMatGaardebounen_Beer.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i><a href="/wiki/Judd_mat_Gaardebounen" title="Judd mat Gaardebounen">Judd mat Gaardebounen</a></i>, served with boiled potatoes and <a href="/wiki/Diekirch" title="Diekirch">Diekirch</a> beer</div></div></div>
<p>Luxembourg cuisine reflects its position on the border between the Latin and Germanic worlds, being heavily influenced by the cuisines of neighboring France and Germany. More recently,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (April 2022)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> it has been enriched by its many Italian and Portuguese immigrants.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Most native Luxembourg dishes, consumed as the traditional daily fare, share roots in the country's folk dishes, the same as in neighboring <a href="/wiki/German_cuisine" title="German cuisine">Germany</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg sells the most alcohol in Europe per capita.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> However, the large proportion of alcohol purchased by customers from neighboring countries contributes to the statistically high level of alcohol sales per capita; this level of alcohol sales is thus not representative of the actual alcohol consumption of the Luxembourg population.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg has the second highest number of <a href="/wiki/Michelin_Guide" title="Michelin Guide">Michelin-starred</a> restaurants per capita with Japan ranked at number one and Switzerland following Luxembourg at number three.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The main languages of media in Luxembourg are French and German. The newspaper with the largest circulation is the German-language daily <i><a href="/wiki/Luxemburger_Wort" title="Luxemburger Wort">Luxemburger Wort</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Because of the strong multilingualism in Luxembourg, newspapers often alternate articles in French and articles in German, without translation. In addition, there are both English and Portuguese radio and national print publications, but accurate audience figures are difficult to gauge since the national media survey by ILRES is conducted in French.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg is known in Europe for its radio and television stations (<a href="/wiki/Radio_Luxembourg_(French)" class="mw-redirect" title="Radio Luxembourg (French)">Radio Luxembourg</a> and <a href="/wiki/RTL_Group" title="RTL Group">RTL Group</a>). It is also the uplink home of <a href="/wiki/SES_S.A." title="SES S.A.">SES</a>, carrier of major European satellite services for Germany and Britain.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to a 1988 law that established a special tax scheme for audiovisual investment, the film and co-production in Luxembourg has grown steadily.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> There are some 30 registered production companies in Luxembourg.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Luxembourg won an <a href="/wiki/Academy_Awards" title="Academy Awards">Oscar</a> in 2014 in the <a href="/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Animated_Short_Film" class="mw-redirect" title="Academy Award for Animated Short Film">Animated Short Films</a> category with <i><a href="/wiki/Mr_Hublot" title="Mr Hublot">Mr Hublot</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_Luxembourgers">Notable Luxembourgers</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Luxembourg&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Notable Luxembourgers">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
